The schema is given below:\\n46:  *\\n47:  * RecordBatch =>\\n48:  *  BaseOffset => Int64\\n49:  *  Length => Int32\\n50:  *  PartitionLeaderEpoch => Int32\\n51:  *  Magic => Int8\\n52:  *  CRC => Uint32\\n53:  *  Attributes => Int16\\n54:  *  LastOffsetDelta => Int32 // also serves as LastSequenceDelta\\n55:  *  FirstTimestamp => Int64\\n56:  *  MaxTimestamp => Int64\\n57:  *  ProducerId => Int64\\n58:  *  ProducerEpoch => Int16\\n59:  *  BaseSequence => Int32\\n60:  *  Records => [Record]\\n61:  *\\n62:  * Note that when compression is enabled (see attributes below), the compressed record data is serialized\\n63:  * directly following the count of the number of records.\\n64:  *\\n65:  * The CRC covers the data from the attributes to the end of the batch (i.e. all the bytes that follow the CRC). It is\\n66:  * located after the magic byte, which means that clients must parse the magic byte before deciding how to interpret\\n67:  * the bytes between the batch length and the magic byte. The partition leader epoch field is not included in the CRC\\n68:  * computation to avoid the need to recompute the CRC when this field is assigned for every batch that is received by\\n69:  * the broker. The CRC-32C (Castagnoli) polynomial is used for the computation.\\n70:  *\\n71:  * On Compaction: Unlike the older message formats, magic v2 and above preserves the first and last offset/sequence\\n72:  * numbers from the original batch when the log is cleaned. This is required in order to be able to restore the\\n73:  * producer's state when the log is reloaded. If we did not retain the last sequence number, then following\\n74:  * a partition leader failure, once the new leader has rebuilt the producer state from the log, the next sequence\\n75:  * expected number would no longer be in sync with what was written by the client. This would cause an\\n76:  * unexpected OutOfOrderSequence error, which is typically fatal. The base sequence number must be preserved for\\n77:  * duplicate checking: the broker checks incoming Produce requests for duplicates by verifying that the first and\\n78:  * last sequence numbers of the incoming batch match the last from that producer.\\n79:  *\\n80:  * Note that if all of the records in a batch are removed during compaction, the broker may still retain an empty\\n81:  * batch header in order to preserve the producer sequence information as described above. These empty batches\\n82:  * are retained only until either a new sequence number is written by the corresponding producer or the producerId\\n83:  * is expired from lack of activity.\\n84:  *\\n85:  * There is no similar need to preserve the timestamp from the original batch after compaction. The FirstTimestamp\\n86:  * field therefore always reflects the timestamp of the first record in the batch. If the batch is empty, the\\n87:  * FirstTimestamp will be set to -1 (NO_TIMESTAMP).\\n88:  *\\n89:  * Similarly, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the maximum timestamp of the current records if the timestamp type\\n90:  * is CREATE_TIME. For LOG_APPEND_TIME, on the other hand, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the timestamp set\\n91:  * by the broker and is preserved after compaction. Additionally, the MaxTimestamp of an empty batch always retains\\n92:  * the previous value prior to becoming empty.\\n93:  *\\n94:  * The delete horizon flag for the sixth bit is used to determine if the first timestamp of the batch had been set to\\n95:  * the time for which tombstones / transaction markers need to be removed. If it is true, then the first timestamp is\\n96:  * the delete horizon, otherwise, it is merely the first timestamp of the record batch.\\n97:  *\\n98:  * The current attributes are given below:\\n99:  *\\n100:  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n101:  *  | Unused (7-15) | Delete Horizon Flag (6) | Control (5) | Transactional (4) | Timestamp Type (3) | Compression Type (0-2) |\\n102:  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n103:  */\\n104: public class DefaultRecordBatch extends AbstractRecordBatch implements MutableRecordBatch {\\n105:     static final int BASE_OFFSET_OFFSET = 0;\\n106:     static final int BASE_OFFSET_LENGTH = 8;\\n107:     static final int LENGTH_OFFSET = 4;\\n119:     static final int BASE_TIMESTAMP_OFFSET = LAST_OFFSET_DELTA_OFFSET + LAST_OFFSET_DELTA_LENGTH;\\n120:     static final int BASE_TIMESTAMP_LENGTH = 8;\\n121:     static final int      }\\n301:             };\\n302:         }\\n303:     }\\n304: \\n305:     private CloseableIterator<Record> uncompressedIterator() {\\n306:         final ByteBuffer buffer = }\\n562: \\n563:     public static int incrementSequence(int sequence, int increment) {\\n564:         if (sequence > Integer.MAX_VALUE - increment)\\n565:             return increment - The schema is given below:\\n46:  *\\n47:  * RecordBatch =>\\n48:  *  BaseOffset => Int64\\n49:  *  Length => Int32\\n50:  *  PartitionLeaderEpoch => Int32\\n51:  *  Magic => Int8\\n52:  *  CRC => Uint32\\n53:  *  Attributes => Int16\\n54:  *  LastOffsetDelta => Int32 // also serves as LastSequenceDelta\\n55:  *  FirstTimestamp => Int64\\n56:  *  MaxTimestamp => Int64\\n57:  *  ProducerId => Int64\\n58:  *  ProducerEpoch => Int16\\n59:  *  BaseSequence => Int32\\n60:  *  Records => [Record]\\n61:  *\\n62:  * Note that when compression is enabled (see attributes below), the compressed record data is serialized\\n63:  * directly following the count of the number of records.\\n64:  *\\n65:  * The CRC covers the data from the attributes to the end of the batch (i.e. all the bytes that follow the CRC). It is\\n66:  * located after the magic byte, which means that clients must parse the magic byte before deciding how to interpret\\n67:  * the bytes between the batch length and the magic byte. The partition leader epoch field is not included in the CRC\\n68:  * computation to avoid the need to recompute the CRC when this field is assigned for every batch that is received by\\n69:  * the broker. The CRC-32C (Castagnoli) polynomial is used for the computation.\\n70:  *\\n71:  * On Compaction: Unlike the older message formats, magic v2 and above preserves the first and last offset/sequence\\n72:  * numbers from the original batch when the log is cleaned. This is required in order to be able to restore the\\n73:  * producer's state when the log is reloaded. If we did not retain the last sequence number, then following\\n74:  * a partition leader failure, once the new leader has rebuilt the producer state from the log, the next sequence\\n75:  * expected number would no longer be in sync with what was written by the client. This would cause an\\n76:  * unexpected OutOfOrderSequence error, which is typically fatal. The base sequence number must be preserved for\\n77:  * duplicate checking: the broker checks incoming Produce requests for duplicates by verifying that the first and\\n78:  * last sequence numbers of the incoming batch match the last from that producer.\\n79:  *\\n80:  * Note that if all of the records in a batch are removed during compaction, the broker may still retain an empty\\n81:  * batch header in order to preserve the producer sequence information as described above. These empty batches\\n82:  * are retained only until either a new sequence number is written by the corresponding producer or the producerId\\n83:  * is expired from lack of activity.\\n84:  *\\n85:  * There is no similar need to preserve the timestamp from the original batch after compaction. The FirstTimestamp\\n86:  * field therefore always reflects the timestamp of the first record in the batch. If the batch is empty, the\\n87:  * FirstTimestamp will be set to -1 (NO_TIMESTAMP).\\n88:  *\\n89:  * Similarly, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the maximum timestamp of the current records if the timestamp type\\n90:  * is CREATE_TIME. For LOG_APPEND_TIME, on the other hand, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the timestamp set\\n91:  * by the broker and is preserved after compaction. Additionally, the MaxTimestamp of an empty batch always retains\\n92:  * the previous value prior to becoming empty.\\n93:  *\\n94:  * The delete horizon flag for the sixth bit is used to determine if the first timestamp of the batch had been set to\\n95:  * the time for which tombstones / transaction markers need to be removed. If it is true, then the first timestamp is\\n96:  * the delete horizon, otherwise, it is merely the first timestamp of the record batch.\\n97:  *\\n98:  * The current attributes are given below:\\n99:  *\\n100:  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n101:  *  | Unused (7-15) | Delete Horizon Flag (6) | Control (5) | Transactional (4) | Timestamp Type (3) | Compression Type (0-2) |\\n102:  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n103:  */\\n104: public class DefaultRecordBatch extends /*\n2:  * Licensed to the Apache Software Foundation (ASF) under one or more\n3:  * contributor license agreements. The schema is given below:\n46:  *\n47:  * RecordBatch =>\n48:  *  BaseOffset => Int64\n49:  *  Length => Int32\n50:  *  PartitionLeaderEpoch => Int32\n51:  *  Magic => Int8\n52:  *  CRC => Uint32\n53:  *  Attributes => Int16\n54:  *  LastOffsetDelta => Int32 // also serves as LastSequenceDelta\n55:  *  FirstTimestamp => Int64\n56:  *  MaxTimestamp => Int64\n57:  *  ProducerId => Int64\n58:  *  ProducerEpoch => Int16\n59:  *  BaseSequence => Int32\n60:  *  Records => [Record]\n61:  *\n62:  * Note that when compression is enabled (see attributes below), the compressed record data is serialized\n63:  * directly following the count of the number of records.\n64:  *\n65:  * The CRC covers the data from the attributes to the end of the batch (i.e. all the bytes that follow the CRC). It is\n66:  * located after the magic byte, which means that clients must parse the magic byte before deciding how to interpret\n67:  * the bytes between the batch length and the magic byte. The partition leader epoch field is not included in the CRC\n68:  * computation to avoid the need to recompute the CRC when this field is assigned for every batch that is received by\n69:  * the broker. The CRC-32C (Castagnoli) polynomial is used for the computation.\n70:  *\n71:  * On Compaction: Unlike the older message formats, magic v2 and above preserves the first and last offset/sequence\n72:  * numbers from the original batch when the log is cleaned. This is required in order to be able to restore the\n73:  * producer's state when the log is reloaded. If we did not retain the last sequence number, then following\n74:  * a partition leader failure, once the new leader has rebuilt the producer state from the log, the next sequence\n75:  * expected number would no longer be in sync with what was written by the client. This would cause an\n76:  * unexpected OutOfOrderSequence error, which is typically fatal. The base sequence number must be preserved for\n77:  * duplicate checking: the broker checks incoming Produce requests for duplicates by verifying that the first and\n78:  * last sequence numbers of the incoming batch match the last from that producer.\n79:  *\n80:  * Note that if all of the records in a batch are removed during compaction, the broker may still retain an empty\n81:  * batch header in order to preserve the producer sequence information as described above. These empty batches\n82:  * are retained only until either a new sequence number is written by the corresponding producer or the producerId\n83:  * is expired from lack of activity.\n84:  *\n85:  * There is no similar need to preserve the timestamp from the original batch after compaction. The FirstTimestamp\n86:  * field therefore always reflects the timestamp of the first record in the batch. If the batch is empty, the\n87:  * FirstTimestamp will be set to -1 (NO_TIMESTAMP).\n88:  *\n89:  * Similarly, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the maximum timestamp of the current records if the timestamp type\n90:  * is CREATE_TIME. For LOG_APPEND_TIME, on the other hand, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the timestamp set\n91:  * by the broker and is preserved after compaction. Additionally, the MaxTimestamp of an empty batch always retains\n92:  * the previous value prior to becoming empty.\n93:  *\n94:  * The delete horizon flag for the sixth bit is used to determine if the first timestamp of the batch had been set to\n95:  * the time for which tombstones / transaction markers need to be removed. If it is true, then the first timestamp is\n96:  * the delete horizon, otherwise, it is merely the first timestamp of the record batch.\n97:  *\n98:  * The current attributes are given below:\n99:  *\n100:  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n101:  *  | Unused (7-15) | Delete Horizon Flag (6) | Control (5) | Transactional (4) | Timestamp Type (3) | Compression Type (0-2) |\n102:  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n103:  */\n104: public class DefaultRecordBatch extends AbstractRecordBatch buffer.limit());\n78: \n79:         return Utils.tryWriteTo(channel, (int) position, length, buffer);\n80:     }\n81: \n82:     /**\n83:      * Write all records to the given channel (including partial records).\n84:      * @param channel The channel to write to\n85:      * @return The number of bytes written\n86:      * @throws IOException For any IO errors writing to the channel\n87:      */\n88:     public int writeFullyTo(GatheringByteChannel channel) throws IOException {\n89:         buffer.mark();\n90:         int written = 0;\n91:         while (written < sizeInBytes())\n92:             written += channel.write(buffer);\n93:         buffer.reset();\n94:         return written;\n95:     }\n96: \n97:     /**\n98:      * The total number of bytes in this message set not including any partial, trailing messages. This\n99:      * may be smaller than what is returned by {@link #sizeInBytes()}.\n100:      * @return The number of valid bytes\n101:      */\n102:     public int validBytes() {\n103:         if (validBytes >= 0)\n104:             return validBytes;\n105: \n106:         int bytes = 0;\n107:         for (RecordBatch batch : batches())\n108:             bytes += batch.sizeInBytes();\n109: \n110:         this.validBytes = bytes;\n111:         return bytes;\n112:     }\n113: \n114:     @Override\n115:     public ConvertedRecords<MemoryRecords> downConvert(byte toMagic, long firstOffset, Time time) {\n116:         return RecordsUtil.downConvert(batches(), toMagic, firstOffset, time);\n117:     }\n118: \n119:     @Override\n120:     public AbstractIterator<MutableRecordBatch> batchIterator() {\n121:         return new RecordBatchIterator<>(new ByteBufferLogInputStream(buffer.duplicate(), Integer.MAX_VALUE));\n122:     }\n123: \n124:     /**\n125:      * Validates the header of the first batch and returns batch size.\n126:      * @return first batch size including LOG_OVERHEAD if buffer contains header up to\n127:      *         magic byte, null otherwise\n128:      * @throws CorruptRecordException if record size or magic is invalid\n129:      */\n130:     public Integer firstBatchSize() {\n131:         if (buffer.remaining() < HEADER_SIZE_UP_TO_MAGIC)\n132:             return null;\n133:         return new ByteBufferLogInputStream(buffer, Integer.MAX_VALUE).nextBatchSize();\n134:     }\n135: \n136:     /**\n137:      * Filter the records into the provided ByteBuffer.\n138:      *\n139:      * @param partition                   The partition that is filtered (used only for logging)\n140:      * @param filter                      The filter function\n141:      * @param destinationBuffer           The byte buffer to write the filtered records to\n142:      * @param maxRecordBatchSize          The maximum record batch size. Note this is not a hard limit: if a batch\n143:      *                                    exceeds this after filtering, we log a warning, but the batch will still be\n144:      *                                    created.\n145:      * @param decompressionBufferSupplier The supplier of ByteBuffer(s) used for decompression if supported. For small\n146:      *                                    record batches, allocating a potentially large buffer (64 KB for LZ4) will\n147:      *                                    dominate the cost of decompressing and iterating over the records in the\n148:      *                                    batch. As such, a supplier that reuses buffers will have a significant\n149:      *                                    performance impact.\n150:      * @return A FilterResult with a summary of the output (for metrics) and potentially an overflow buffer\n151:      */\n152:     public FilterResult filterTo(TopicPartition partition, RecordFilter filter, ByteBuffer destinationBuffer,\n153:                                  int maxRecordBatchSize, BufferSupplier decompressionBufferSupplier) {\n154:         return filterTo(partition, batches(), filter, destinationBuffer, maxRecordBatchSize, decompressionBufferSupplier);\n155:     }\n156: \n157:     /**\n158:      * Note: This method is also used to convert the first timestamp of the batch (which is usually the timestamp of the first record)\n159:      * to the delete horizon of the tombstones or txn markers which are present in the batch. \n160:      */\n161:     private static FilterResult filterTo(TopicPartition partition, Iterable<MutableRecordBatch> batches,\n162:                                          RecordFilter filter, ByteBuffer destinationBuffer, int maxRecordBatchSize,\n163:                                          BufferSupplier decompressionBufferSupplier) {\n164:         FilterResult filterResult = new FilterResult(destinationBuffer);\n165:         ByteBufferOutputStream bufferOutputStream = new ByteBufferOutputStream(destinationBuffer);\n166:         for (MutableRecordBatch batch : batches) {\n167:             final BatchRetentionResult batchRetentionResult = filter.checkBatchRetention(batch);\n168:             final boolean containsMarkerForEmptyTxn = batchRetentionResult.containsMarkerForEmptyTxn;\n169:             final BatchRetention batchRetention = batchRetentionResult.batchRetention;\n170: \n171:             filterResult.bytesRead += batch.sizeInBytes();\n172: \n173:             if (batchRetention == BatchRetention.DELETE)\n174:                 continue;\n175: \n176:             // We use the absolute offset to decide whether to retain the message or not. Due to KAFKA-4298, we have to\n177:             // allow for the possibility that a previous version corrupted the log by writing a compressed record batch\n178:             // with a magic value not matching the magic of the records (magic < 2). This will be fixed as we\n179:             // recopy the messages to the destination buffer.\n180:             byte batchMagic = batch.magic();\n181:             List<Record> retainedRecords = new ArrayList<>();\n182: \n183:             final BatchFilterResult iterationResult = filterBatch(batch, decompressionBufferSupplier, filterResult, filter,\n184:                     batchMagic, true, retainedRecords);\n185:             boolean containsTombstones = iterationResult.containsTombstones;\n186:             boolean writeOriginalBatch = iterationResult.writeOriginalBatch;\n187:             long maxOffset = iterationResult.maxOffset;\n188: \n189:             if (!retainedRecords.isEmpty()) {\n190:                 // we check if the delete horizon should be set to a new value\n191:                 // in which case, we need to reset the base timestamp and overwrite the timestamp deltas\n192:                 // if the batch does not contain tombstones, then we don't need to overwrite batch\n193:                 boolean needToSetDeleteHorizon = batch.magic() >= RecordBatch.MAGIC_VALUE_V2 && (containsTombstones || containsMarkerForEmptyTxn)\n194:                     && !batch.deleteHorizonMs().isPresent();\n195:                 if (writeOriginalBatch && !needToSetDeleteHorizon) {\n196:                     batch.writeTo(bufferOutputStream);\n197:                     filterResult.updateRetainedBatchMetadata(batch, retainedRecords.size(), false);\n198:                 } else {\n199:                     final MemoryRecordsBuilder builder;\n200:                     long deleteHorizonMs;\n201:                     if (needToSetDeleteHorizon)\n202:                         deleteHorizonMs = filter.currentTime + filter.deleteRetentionMs;\n203:                     else\n204:                         deleteHorizonMs = batch.deleteHorizonMs().orElse(RecordBatch.NO_TIMESTAMP);\n205:                     builder = buildRetainedRecordsInto(batch, retainedRecords, bufferOutputStream, deleteHorizonMs);\n206: \n207:                     MemoryRecords records = builder.build();\n208:                     int filteredBatchSize = records.sizeInBytes();\n209:                     if (filteredBatchSize > batch.sizeInBytes() && filteredBatchSize > maxRecordBatchSize)\n210:                         log.warn(\"Record batch from {} with last offset {} exceeded max record batch size {} after cleaning \" +\n211:                                         \"(new size is {}). Consumers with version earlier than 0.10.1.0 may need to \" +\n212:                                         \"increase their fetch sizes.\",\n213:                                 partition, batch.lastOffset(), maxRecordBatchSize, filteredBatchSize);\n214: \n215:                     MemoryRecordsBuilder.RecordsInfo info = builder.info();\n216:                     filterResult.updateRetainedBatchMetadata(info.maxTimestamp, info.shallowOffsetOfMaxTimestamp,\n217:                             maxOffset, retainedRecords.size(), filteredBatchSize);\n218:                 }\n219:             } else if (batchRetention == BatchRetention.RETAIN_EMPTY) {\n220:                 if (batchMagic < RecordBatch.MAGIC_VALUE_V2)\n221:                     throw new IllegalStateException(\"Empty batches are only supported for magic v2 and above\");\n222: \n223:                 bufferOutputStream.ensureRemaining(DefaultRecordBatch.RECORD_BATCH_OVERHEAD);\n224:                 DefaultRecordBatch.writeEmptyHeader(bufferOutputStream.buffer(), batchMagic, batch.producerId(),\n225:                         batch.producerEpoch(), batch.baseSequence(), batch.baseOffset(), batch.lastOffset(),\n226:                         batch.partitionLeaderEpoch(), batch.timestampType(), batch.maxTimestamp(),\n227:                         batch.isTransactional(), batch.isControlBatch());\n228:                 filterResult.updateRetainedBatchMetadata(batch, 0, true);\n229:             }\n230: \n231:             // If we had to allocate a new buffer to fit the filtered buffer (see KAFKA-5316), return early to\n232:             // avoid the need for additional allocations.\n233:             ByteBuffer outputBuffer = bufferOutputStream.buffer();\n234:             if (outputBuffer != destinationBuffer) {\n235:                 filterResult.outputBuffer = outputBuffer;\n236:                 return filterResult;\n237:             }\n238:         }\n239: \n240:         return filterResult;\n241:     }\n242: \n243:     private static BatchFilterResult filterBatch(RecordBatch batch,\n244:                                                  BufferSupplier decompressionBufferSupplier,\n245:                                                  FilterResult filterResult,\n246:                                                  RecordFilter filter,\n247:                                                  byte batchMagic,\n248:                                                  boolean writeOriginalBatch,\n249:                                                  List<Record> retainedRecords) {\n250:         long maxOffset = -1;\n251:         boolean containsTombstones = false;\n252:         try (final CloseableIterator<Record> iterator = batch.streamingIterator(decompressionBufferSupplier)) {\n253:             while (iterator.hasNext()) {\n254:                 Record record = iterator.next();\n255:                 filterResult.messagesRead += 1;\n256: \n257:                 if (filter.shouldRetainRecord(batch, record)) {\n258:                     // Check for log corruption due to KAFKA-4298. If we find it, make sure that we overwrite\n259:                     // the corrupted batch with correct data.\n260:                     if (!record.hasMagic(batchMagic))\n261:                         writeOriginalBatch = false;\n262: \n263:                     if (record.offset() > maxOffset)\n264:                         maxOffset = record.offset();\n265: \n266:                     retainedRecords.add(record);\n267: \n268:                     if (!record.hasValue()) {\n269:                         containsTombstones = true;\n270:                     }\n271:                 } else {\n272:                     writeOriginalBatch = false;\n273:                 }\n274:             }\n275:             return new BatchFilterResult(writeOriginalBatch, containsTombstones, maxOffset);\n276:         }\n277:     }\n278: \n279:     private static class BatchFilterResult {\n280:         private final boolean writeOriginalBatch;\n281:         private final boolean containsTombstones;\n282:         private final long maxOffset;\n283:         private BatchFilterResult(final boolean writeOriginalBatch,\n284:                                  final boolean containsTombstones,\n285:                                  final long maxOffset) {\n286:             this.writeOriginalBatch = writeOriginalBatch;\n287:             this.containsTombstones = containsTombstones;\n288:             this.maxOffset = maxOffset;\n289:         }\n290:     }\n291: \n292:     private static MemoryRecordsBuilder buildRetainedRecordsInto(RecordBatch originalBatch,\n293:                                                                  List<Record> retainedRecords,\n294:                                                                  ByteBufferOutputStream bufferOutputStream,\n295:                                                                  final long deleteHorizonMs) {\n296:         byte magic = originalBatch.magic();\n297:         TimestampType timestampType = originalBatch.timestampType();\n298:         long logAppendTime = timestampType == TimestampType.LOG_APPEND_TIME ?\n299:                 originalBatch.maxTimestamp() : RecordBatch.NO_TIMESTAMP;\n300:         long baseOffset = magic >= RecordBatch.MAGIC_VALUE_V2 ?\n301:                 originalBatch.baseOffset() : retainedRecords.get(0).offset();\n302: \n303:         MemoryRecordsBuilder builder = new MemoryRecordsBuilder(bufferOutputStream, magic,\n304:                 originalBatch.compressionType(), timestampType, baseOffset, logAppendTime, originalBatch.producerId(),\n305:                 originalBatch.producerEpoch(), originalBatch.baseSequence(), originalBatch.isTransactional(),\n306:                 originalBatch.isControlBatch(), originalBatch.partitionLeaderEpoch(), bufferOutputStream.limit(), deleteHorizonMs);\n307: \n308:         for (Record record : retainedRecords)\n309:             builder.append(record);\n310: \n311:         if (magic >= RecordBatch.MAGIC_VALUE_V2)\n312:             // we must preserve the last offset from the initial batch in order to ensure that the\n313:             // last sequence number from the batch remains even after compaction. Otherwise, the producer\n314:             // could incorrectly see an out of sequence error.\n315:             builder.overrideLastOffset(originalBatch.lastOffset());\n316: \n317:         return builder;\n318:     }\n319: \n320:     /**\n321:      * Get the byte buffer that backs this instance for reading.\n322:      */\n323:     public ByteBuffer buffer() {\n324:         return buffer.duplicate();\n325:     }\n326: \n327:     @Override\n328:     public Iterable<MutableRecordBatch> batches() {\n329:         return batches;\n330:     }\n331: \n332:     @Override\n333: {\n353:         return buffer.hashCode();\n354:     }\n355: \n356:     public static abstract class RecordFilter {\n357:         public final long currentTime;\n358:         public final long     }\n374: \n375:         public enum BatchRetention {\n376:             DELETE, // Delete the batch without inspecting records\n377:             RETAIN_EMPTY, // Retain the batch even if it is DefaultRecordBatch Implementation Enhancement\n\n### File: `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/record/DefaultRecordBatch.java`\n\n```diff\n--- a/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/record/DefaultRecordBatch.java\n+++ b/clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/record/DefaultRecordBatch.java\n@@ -44,13 +44,72 @@ import static org.apache.kafka.common.record.Records.LOG_OVERHEAD;\n /**\n  * RecordBatch implementation for magic 2 and above. The schema is given below:\n  *\n- * RecordBatch =>\\n\n  *  BaseOffset => Int64\\n\n  *  Length => Int32\\n\n  *  PartitionLeaderEpoch => Int32\\n\n  *  Magic => Int8\\n\n  *  CRC => Uint32\\n\n  *  Attributes => Int16\\n\n  *  LastOffsetDelta => Int32 // also serves as LastSequenceDelta\\n\n  *  FirstTimestamp => Int64\\n\n  *  MaxTimestamp => Int64\\n\n  *  ProducerId => Int64\\n\n  *  ProducerEpoch => Int16\\n\n  *  BaseSequence => Int32\\n\n  *  Records => [Record]\\n\n  *\n  * Note that when compression is enabled (see attributes below), the compressed record data is serialized\n  * directly following the count of the number of records.\n  *\n  * The CRC covers the data from the attributes to the end of the batch (i.e. all the bytes that follow the CRC). It is\n  * located after the magic byte, which means that clients must parse the magic byte before deciding how to interpret\n  * the bytes between the batch length and the magic byte. The partition leader epoch field is not included in the CRC\n  * computation to avoid the need to recompute the CRC when this field is assigned for every batch that is received by\n  * the broker. The CRC-32C (Castagnoli) polynomial is used for the computation.\n  *\n  * On Compaction: Unlike the older message formats, magic v2 and above preserves the first and last offset/sequence\n  * numbers from the original batch when the log is cleaned. This is required in order to be able to restore the\n  * producer's state when the log is reloaded. If we did not retain the last sequence number, then following\n  * a partition leader failure, once the new leader has rebuilt the producer state from the log, the next sequence\n  * expected number would no longer be in sync with what was written by the client. This would cause an\n  * unexpected OutOfOrderSequence error, which is typically fatal. The base sequence number must be preserved for\n  * duplicate checking: the broker checks incoming Produce requests for duplicates by verifying that the first and\n  * last sequence numbers of the incoming batch match the last from that producer.\n  *\n  * Note that if all of the records in a batch are removed during compaction, the broker may still retain an empty\n  * batch header in order to preserve the producer sequence information as described above. These empty batches\n  * are retained only until either a new sequence number is written by the corresponding producer or the producerId\n  * is expired from lack of activity.\n  *\n  * There is no similar need to preserve the timestamp from the original batch after compaction. The FirstTimestamp\n  * field therefore always reflects the timestamp of the first record in the batch. If the batch is empty, the\n  * FirstTimestamp will be set to -1 (NO_TIMESTAMP).\n  *\n  * Similarly, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the maximum timestamp of the current records if the timestamp type\n  * is CREATE_TIME. For LOG_APPEND_TIME, on the other hand, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the timestamp set\n  * by the broker and is preserved after compaction. Additionally, the MaxTimestamp of an empty batch always retains\n  * the previous value prior to becoming empty.\n  *\n  * The delete horizon flag for the sixth bit is used to determine if the first timestamp of the batch had been set to\n  * the time for which tombstones / transaction markers need to be removed. If it is true, then the first timestamp is\n  * the delete horizon, otherwise, it is merely the first timestamp of the record batch.\n  *\n  * The current attributes are given below:\n  *\n  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n  *  | Unused (7-15) | Delete Horizon Flag (6) | Control (5) | Transactional (4) | Timestamp Type (3) | Compression Type (0-2) |\n  *  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n+ *\n+ * <h3>Thread Safety</h3>\n+ * <strong>Not thread-safe.</strong> This class wraps a {@link java.nio.ByteBuffer} which is not thread-safe.\n+ * The underlying buffer is typically read-only after construction, but the class does support mutable operations\n+ * (e.g., {@link #setMaxTimestamp(TimestampType, long)}, {@link #setPartitionLeaderEpoch(int)}) which can be used\n+ * during log compaction. Any concurrent access requires external synchronization.\n+ *\n+ * <h3>Performance Characteristics</h3>\n+ * <ul>\n+ * <li><strong>Header access (O(1)):</strong> All metadata fields (offset, timestamp, producer ID, CRC) are accessed\n+ *     directly from the underlying buffer with constant time complexity</li>\n+ * <li><strong>Record iteration:</strong> Uncompressed batches iterate in O(n) time. The caller must\n+     * ensure exclusive access to the buffer during the write operation.\n+     *\n+     * <p><strong>CRC Computation:</strong> The CRC-32C (Castagnoli) algorithm is used, covering bytes from\n+     * ATTRIBUTES_OFFSET to the end of the provided size range. It is\n  * located after the magic byte, which means that clients must parse the magic byte before deciding how to interpret\n  * the bytes between the batch length and the magic byte. The partition leader epoch field is not included in the CRC\n  * computation to avoid the need to recompute the CRC when this field is assigned for every batch that is received by\n  * the broker. The CRC-32C (Castagnoli) polynomial is used for the computation.\n  *\n  * On Compaction: Unlike the older message formats, magic v2 and above preserves the first and last offset/sequence\n  * numbers from the original batch when the log is cleaned. This is required in order to be able to restore the\n  * producer's state when the log is reloaded. If we did not retain the last sequence number, then following\n  * a partition leader failure, once the new leader has rebuilt the producer state from the log, the next sequence\n  * expected number would no longer be in sync with what was written by the client. This would cause an\n  * unexpected OutOfOrderSequence error, which is typically fatal. The base sequence number must be preserved for\n  * duplicate checking: the broker checks incoming Produce requests for duplicates by verifying that the first and\n  * last sequence numbers of the incoming batch match the last from that producer.\n  *\n  * Note that if all of the records in a batch are removed during compaction, the broker may still retain an empty\n  * batch header in order to preserve the producer sequence information as described above. These empty batches\n  * are retained only until either a new sequence number is written by the corresponding producer or the producerId\n  * is expired from lack of activity.\n  *\n  * There is no similar need to preserve the timestamp from the original batch after compaction. The FirstTimestamp\n  * field therefore always reflects the timestamp of the first record in the batch. If the batch is empty, the\n  * FirstTimestamp will be set to -1 (NO_TIMESTAMP).\n  *\n  * Similarly, the MaxTimestamp field reflects the maximum timestamp of the current records if the timestamp type\n  * is CREATE_TIME.
